. For every fact
P(\bar{a},b)
such that there is a fact
T(\bar{a},b,c)
in
D
, we add the fact
R(\bar{a},b,c)
to
E
. Similarly to the previous lemmas, We can show that
\mathsf{rfreq}_{D,\Sigma^{\prime}}(Q^{\prime})=\mathsf{rfreq}_{E,\Sigma}(Q)

and that
Q^{\prime}
is not
\Sigma^{\prime}
-safe.

Lemma

16
.

If for the
R
-atom of
Q
for some relation name
R
it holds that

1.

\Sigma_{R}
has a single FD
R:X\rightarrow\mathord{\{A\}}
,

2.

there are only two non-primary-FD positions
(R,A^{\prime})
and
(R,A^{\prime\prime})
, and

3.

the position
(R,A)
is associated with an orphan variable
x
and
(R,A^{\prime}),(R,A^{\prime\prime})
with the same liaison variable
y
that occurs only in
\alpha
,

then
\mathsf{RelFreq}(\Sigma^{\prime},Q^{\prime})
is Cook reducible to
\mathsf{RelFreq}(\Sigma,Q)
for some
\Sigma^{\prime}
and an SJFCQ
Q^{\prime}
that is not
\Sigma^{\prime}
-safe, where
n-1
atoms of
Q^{\prime}
are non-single-key atoms w.r.t.
\Sigma^{\prime}
.

Proof.

Assume, without loss of generality, that
\alpha=R(\bar{z},x,y,y)
.
Let
Q^{\prime}=Q\setminus\mathord{\{R(\bar{z},x,y,y)\}}\cup\mathord{\{P(\bar{z},x),T(\bar{z},x,y,y)\}}
, where
P
and
T
are two fresh relation names that do not occur in
Q
. We denote by
\Sigma^{\prime}
the FD set obtained from
\Sigma
by removing the FDs of
\Sigma_{R}
and adding the FDs
P:(\mathsf{att}(P)\setminus\mathord{\{B\}})\rightarrow\mathord{\{B\}}
and
T:\mathsf{att}(T)\rightarrow\emptyset
, where
B
is the attribute associated with the variable
x
in
P(\bar{z},x)
.

Given a database
D
over
\mathbf{S}^{\prime}
, we construct a database
E
over
\mathbf{S}
by adding to
E
all the
R^{\prime}
-facts of
D
for relations
R^{\prime}
that occur in both
\mathbf{S}
and
\mathbf{S}^{\prime}
. Moreover, for every fact
P(\bar{a},b)
such that there is no fact of the form
T(\bar{a},b,c,c)
, for some constant
c
, in
D
we add a fact
R(\bar{a},b,\odot,\otimes)
to
E
for constants
\odot\neq\otimes
that do not occur in
D
. For every fact
P(\bar{a},b)
such that there is a fact of the form
T(\bar{a},b,c,c)
in
D
, we add the fact
R(\bar{a},b,c,c)
to
E
. (If there are multiple facts of the form
T(\bar{a},b,*,*)
for some
\bar{a}
we also add multiple facts to
R
, and these facts do not jointly violate
\Sigma_{R}
.)

We can show that
\mathsf{rfreq}_{D,\Sigma^{\prime}}(Q^{\prime})=\mathsf{rfreq}_{E,\Sigma}(Q)

and that
Q^{\prime}
is not
\Sigma^{\prime}
-safe.
The proof is similar to the proofs of the previous lemmas. An important observation here is that a homomorphism
h
from
Q
to a repair
J^{\prime}
of
E
is such that
R(h(\bar{z},x,y,y))\in J^{\prime}
; hence, it cannot be the case that
R(h(\bar{z},x,y,y))
is a fact of the form
R(\bar{a},b,\odot,\otimes)
, but rather a fact of the form
R(\bar{a},b,c,c)
for some constant
c
. By the definition of
J^{\prime}
,
P(h(\bar{z},x))\in J
and
T(h(\bar{z},x,y,y))\in J
, and
h
is a homomorphism from
Q^{\prime}
to
J
.

Lemma

17
.

If for the
R
-atom
\alpha
of
Q
for some relation name
R
it holds that

1.

\Sigma_{R}
has two FDs
R:X\rightarrow\mathord{\{A\}}
,
R:(X\cup\mathord{\{A^{\prime}\}})\rightarrow\mathord{\{A^{\prime\prime}\}}
with the first being the primary FD,

2.

there are no non-primary-lhs positions besides
(R,A)
,
(R,A^{\prime})
,
(R,A^{\prime\prime})
(where
(R,A^{\prime})
and
(R,A^{\prime\prime})
are also non-primary-rhs positions), and

3.

the position
(R,A)
is associated with an orphan variable
x
and
(R,A^{\prime}),(R,A^{\prime\prime})
with the same liaison variable
y
that occurs only in the atom
\alpha
,

then
\mathsf{RelFreq}(\Sigma^{\prime},Q^{\prime})
is Cook reducible to
\mathsf{RelFreq}(\Sigma,Q)
for some
\Sigma^{\prime}
and an SJFCQ
Q^{\prime}
that is not
\Sigma^{\prime}
-safe, where
n-1
atoms of
Q^{\prime}
are non-single-key atoms w.r.t.
\Sigma^{\prime}
.

Proof.

Assume, without loss of generality, that
\alpha=R(\bar{z},x,y,y)
.
Let
Q^{\prime}=Q\setminus\mathord{\{R(\bar{z},x,y,y)\}}\cup\mathord{\{P(\bar{z},x),T(\bar{z},x,y,y)\}}
, where
P,T
are two fresh relation names not in
Q
. We denote by
\Sigma^{\prime}
the FD set obtained from
\Sigma
by removing the FDs of
\Sigma_{R}
and adding the FDs
P:(\mathsf{att}(P)\setminus\mathord{\{B\}})\rightarrow\mathord{\{B\}}
and
T:\mathsf{att}(T)\rightarrow\emptyset
, where
B
is the attribute associated with the variable
x
in
P(\bar{z},x)
.

Given a database
D
over
\mathbf{S}^{\prime}
, we construct a database
E
over
\mathbf{S}
by adding to
E
all the
R^{\prime}
-facts of
D
for relations
R^{\prime}
that occur in both
\mathbf{S}
and
\mathbf{S}^{\prime}
. Moreover, for every fact
P(\bar{a},b)
such that there is no fact of the form
T(\bar{a},b,c,c)
, for some constant
c
, in
D
we add a fact
R(\bar{a},b,b,\odot)
to
E
for a constant
\odot
not in
D
. For every fact
P(\bar{a},b)
such that there is a fact of the form
T(\bar{a},b,c,c)
in
D
, we add the fact
R(\bar{a},b,c,c)
to
E
. If there are multiple facts of the form
T(\bar{a},b,*,*)
for some
\bar{a}
we also add multiple facts to
R
. Note that there are no violations of the FD
R:(X\cup\mathord{\{A^{\prime}\}})\rightarrow\mathord{\{A^{\prime\prime}\}}
in
E
as no two facts of
E
agree on the values of all the attributes in
X\cup\mathord{\{A^{\prime}\}}
.

We can show that
\mathsf{rfreq}_{D,\Sigma^{\prime}}(Q^{\prime})=\mathsf{rfreq}_{E,\Sigma}(Q)

and that
Q^{\prime}
is not
\Sigma^{\prime}
-safe.
The proof is similar to the proofs of the previous lemmas. Observe that a homomorphism
h
from
Q
to a repair
J^{\prime}
of
E
is such that
R(h(\bar{z},x,y,y))\in J^{\prime}
; hence, it cannot be the case that
R(h(\bar{z},x,y,y))
is a fact of the form
R(\bar{a},b,b,\odot)
, but rather a fact of the form
R(\bar{a},b,c,c)
for some constant
c
. By the definition of
J^{\prime}
,
P(h(\bar{z},x))\in J
and
T(h(\bar{z},x,y,y))\in J
, and
h
is a homomorphism from
Q^{\prime}
to
J
.

Appendix B
Proofs of Section
6

B.1
Proof of Proposition
6

Proposition

6
.

Consider a set
\Sigma
of FDs with an LHS chain (up to equivalence), and a CQ
Q
. Given a database
D
with
\mathsf{hom}_{D,\Sigma}(Q)\neq\emptyset
, the following hold for each
H\in\mathsf{hom}_{D,\Sigma}(Q)
:

1.

We can compute
\sharp\mathsf{rep}_{\Sigma,H}(D)
in polynomial time in
||D||
.

2.

We can sample elements of
\mathsf{rep}_{\Sigma,H}(D)
uniformly at random in polynomial time in
||D||
.

3.

Given a database
D^{\prime}
, we can check whether
D^{\prime}\in\mathsf{rep}_{\Sigma,H}(D)
in polynomial time in
||D||
.

Proof.

The proofs for items (1) and (3) have been given in the main body of the paper. We proceed to establish item (2).
As discussed in the proof of item (1),
\mathsf{rep}_{\Sigma,H}(D)=\mathsf{rep}_{\Sigma}(D\setminus D^{\prime})
, where
D^{\prime}=\{f\in D\mid H\cup\{f\}\not\models\Sigma\}
. Thus, sampling repairs from
\mathsf{rep}_{\Sigma,H}(D)
uniformly at random in polynomial time boils down to sampling repairs from
\mathsf{rep}_{\Sigma}(D\setminus D^{\prime})
uniformly at random in polynomial time. Therefore, to prove item (2), we only need to prove the following lemma:

Lemma 43
.

Consider a set of FDs
\Sigma
with an LHS chain (up to equivalence). There exists a randomized procedure
\mathsf{Sample}
that accepts as input
D
and
\Sigma
such that the following hold:

1.

For each
D^{\prime}\in\mathsf{rep}_{\Sigma}(D)
,
\Pr(\mathsf{Sample}(D,\Sigma)=D^{\prime})=\frac{1}{\sharp\mathsf{rep}_{\Sigma}(D)}
.

2.

\mathsf{Sample}(D,\Sigma)
runs in polynomial time in
||D||
.

Proof.

We first need some auxiliary notions and results. Consider a database
D
, a set
\Sigma
of FDs, and a partition
P=\{D_{1},\ldots,D_{n}\}
of
D
. We say that
P
is
\Sigma
-independent
(resp.,
\Sigma
-conflicting
) if for every distinct
i,j\in[n]
,
f\in D_{i}
and
g\in D_{j}
implies that
\{f,g\}\models\Sigma
(resp.,
\{f,g\}\not\models\Sigma
).
We first establish a useful technical result for the case where
P
is
\Sigma
-independent.

Lemma 44
.

Assume that
P
is
\Sigma
-independent. For every database
D^{\prime}
, the following are equivalent:

1.

D^{\prime}\in\mathsf{rep}_{\Sigma}(D)
.

2.

D^{\prime}=\bigcup_{i\in[n]}D^{\prime}_{i}
, where
D^{\prime}_{i}\in\mathsf{rep}_{\Sigma}(D_{i})
for each
i\in[n]
.

Proof.

We first establish the direction
(1)
implies
(2)
. Assume that
D^{\prime}\in\mathsf{rep}_{\Sigma}(D)
. Let
D^{\prime}_{i}
be the restriction of
D^{\prime}
to the facts in
D_{i}
, for each
i\in[n]
. We show that
D^{\prime}_{i}\in\mathsf{rep}_{\Sigma}(D_{i})
, for each
i\in[n]
. Consider an arbitrary
i\in[n]
. Since
D^{\prime}\models\Sigma
, then
D^{\prime}_{i}\models\Sigma
. It remains to show that
D^{\prime}_{i}
is maximal.
Assume, towards a contradiction, that there is a fact
f\in D_{i}\setminus D^{\prime}_{i}
such that
D^{\prime}_{i}\cup\{f\}\models\Sigma
. Since
P
is a
\Sigma
-independent partition, no fact
g\in D^{\prime}_{j}
, for
j\in[n]
and
j\neq i
, is such that
\{f,g\}\not\models\Sigma
. Hence,
D^{\prime}\cup\{f\}\ \models\Sigma
. This contradicts the fact that
D^{\prime}
is a repair of
\mathsf{rep}_{\Sigma}(D)
, and the claim follows.

We now establish
(2)
implies
(1)
. Assume that
D^{\prime}=\bigcup_{i\in[n]}D^{\prime}_{i}
, where
D^{\prime}_{i}\in\mathsf{rep}_{\Sigma}(D_{i})
for each
i\in[n]
. Assume, towards a contradiction, that
D^{\prime}\not\in\mathsf{rep}_{\Sigma}(D)
. Note that
D^{\prime}
is consistent, i.e.,
D^{\prime}\models\Sigma
, since
P
is
\Sigma
-independent. Hence, the fact that
D^{\prime}\not\in\mathsf{rep}_{\Sigma}(D)
implies that
D^{\prime}
is not maximal, i.e., there is a fact
f\in D\setminus D^{\prime}
such that
D^{\prime}\cup\{f\}\models\Sigma
.
However, since
D^{\prime}_{i}\subseteq D^{\prime}
, for each
i\in[n]
, we conclude that
D^{\prime}_{i}\cup\{f\}\models\Sigma
. The latter holds also if
i\in[n]
is such that
f\in D_{i}
. This implies that
D^{\prime}_{i}
is not a repair of
\mathsf{rep}_{\Sigma}(D_{i})
, which contradicts our hypothesis.

We now proceed to establish an analogous technical result for the case where
P
is
\Sigma
-conflicting.

Lemma 45
.

Assume that
P
is
\Sigma
-conflicting. For every database
D^{\prime}
, the following are equivalent:

1.

D^{\prime}\in\mathsf{rep}_{\Sigma}(D)
.

2.

D^{\prime}\in\mathsf{rep}_{\Sigma}(D_{i})
for some
i\in[n]
.

Proof.

We first establish
(1)
implies
(2)
. Assume that
D^{\prime}\in\mathsf{rep}_{\Sigma}(D)
. Since
P
is a
\Sigma
-conflicting partition, and since
D^{\prime}\models\Sigma
,
D^{\prime}
must necessarily contain only facts that all belong to some
D_{i}
, i.e.,
D^{\prime}\subseteq D_{i}
, for some
i\in[n]
. Moreover, since
D^{\prime}\in\mathsf{rep}_{\Sigma}(D)
, for every fact
f\in D\setminus D^{\prime}
,
D^{\prime}\cup\{f\}\not\models\Sigma
. This also holds if
f\in D_{i}
, and therefore
D^{\prime}\in\mathsf{rep}_{\Sigma}(D_{i})
, as needed.

We now establish
(2)
implies
(1)
. Assume that
D^{\prime}\in\mathsf{rep}_{\Sigma}(D_{i})
for some
i\in[n]
. Since
D^{\prime}
is a repair of
D_{i}
w.r.t.
\Sigma
and
P
is a
\Sigma
-conflicting partition of
D
, for every fact
f\in D\setminus D^{\prime}
it holds that
D^{\prime}\cup\{f\}\not\models\Sigma
. Hence,
D^{\prime}\in\mathsf{rep}_{\Sigma}(D)
, and the claim follows.

The following is an easy consequence of Lemmas
44
and
45
:

Corollary 1
.

Consider a database
D
, a set of FDs
\Sigma
, and a partition
P=\{D_{1},\ldots,D_{n}\}
of
D
.

1.

If
P
is
\Sigma
-independent, then
\sharp\mathsf{rep}_{\Sigma}(D)=\prod_{i\in[n]}\sharp\mathsf{rep}_{\Sigma}(D_{i})
.

2.

If
P
is
\Sigma
-conflicting, then
\sharp\mathsf{rep}_{\Sigma}(D)=\sum_{i\in[n]}\sharp\mathsf{rep}_{\Sigma}(D_{i})
.

By exploiting the above results and the notion of blocktree, we show that we can sample repairs of a single-relation database when considering sets of FDs with an LHS chain.

Input:

A database
D
over
\mathbf{S}
, a set
\Sigma
of FDs with an LHS chain over
\mathbf{S}
, and
R\in\mathbf{S}

Output:

D^{\prime}\in\mathsf{rep}_{\Sigma_{R}}(D_{R})
with probability
\frac{1}{\sharp\mathsf{rep}_{\Sigma_{R}}(D_{R})}

1

Let
\Lambda=\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{n}
be an LHS chain of
\Sigma_{R}

Construct the
(R,\Lambda)
-blocktree
T=(V,E,\lambda)
of
D
w.r.t
\Sigma

Let
u_{r}
be the root of
T

return

\mathsf{SampleFromTree}(T,u_{r})

2

Function

\mathsf{SampleFromTree}(T=(V,E,\lambda),u)
:

3

if

u
is a leaf node of
T

then

4

return

\lambda(u)
;

5

else

6

repair
\leftarrow\emptyset
;

7

for

v\in\mathsf{child}(u)

do

8

Choose
v^{\prime}\in\mathsf{child}(v)
with probability
\frac{\sharp\mathsf{rep}_{\Sigma_{R}}(\lambda(v^{\prime}))}{\sharp\mathsf{rep}_{\Sigma_{R}}(\lambda(v))}

D_{v^{\prime}}

\leftarrow

\mathsf{SampleFromTree}(T,v^{\prime})

repair
\leftarrow
repair
\cup D_{v^{\prime}}

return
repair;

9

10

11

Algorithm 3

The procedure
\mathsf{RSample}

Lemma 46
.

Consider a database
D
, and a set of FDs
\Sigma
with an LHS chain, both over a schema
\mathbf{S}
. There is a randomized procedure
\mathsf{RSample}
that takes as input
D
,
\Sigma
and
R\in\mathbf{S}
such that:

1.

For each
D^{\prime}\in\mathsf{rep}_{\Sigma_{R}}(D_{R})
,
\Pr(\mathsf{RSample}(D,\Sigma,R)=D^{\prime})=\frac{1}{\sharp\mathsf{rep}_{\Sigma_{R}}(D_{R})}
.

2.

\mathsf{RSample}(D,\Sigma,R)
runs in polynomial time in
||D||
.

Proof.

Consider the randomized procedure
\mathsf{RSample}
shown in Algorithm
3
. It first constructs the
(R,\Lambda)
-blocktree
T=(V,E,\lambda)
of
D
w.r.t.
\Sigma
, for some LHS chain
\Lambda=\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{n}
of
\Sigma_{R}
. Then, it executes the auxiliary procedure
\mathsf{SampleFromTree}
, with input
T
and its root, and returns the result of this call; here, for a node
u
of
T
, we use
\mathsf{child}(u)
to denote the set of children of
u
in
T
.

Correctness of
\mathsf{RSample}

We first show that
\mathsf{RSample}
is correct, i.e., item (1) of Lemma
46
holds.
To this end, we only need to show that for every node
u
of
T
at an even level,
\mathsf{SampleFromTree}(T,u)
outputs a repair
D^{\prime}\in\mathsf{rep}_{\Sigma_{R}}(\lambda(u))
with probability
\frac{1}{\sharp\mathsf{rep}_{\Sigma_{R}}(\lambda(u))}
. This, since the root
u_{r}
of
T
is at level
0
, and
\lambda(u_{r})=D_{R}
, will immediately imply correctness.
We proceed by induction on the level of
u
.

Base Case.

Assume that
u
is a leaf node of
T
, i.e., at level
2n
. In this case,
\mathsf{SampleFromTree}
returns
\lambda(u)
with probability
1
. Since
u
is a leaf of
T
,
\lambda(u)
is consistent w.r.t.
\Sigma_{R}
. Hence,
\lambda(u)
is the only repair in
\mathsf{rep}_{\Sigma_{R}}(\lambda(u))
, and the claim follows.

Inductive Step.

Assume now that
u
is a node of
T
that appears at level
2i
, for some
i\in\{0,,\ldots,n-1\}
. Assuming
\mathsf{child}(u)=\{v_{1},\ldots,v_{k}\}
, since
u
occurs at an even level, we have that
\{\lambda(v_{1}),\ldots,\lambda(v_{k})\}=\mathsf{block}_{\phi_{i+1}}(\lambda(u))
. That is, each child of
u
is associated to a block of the database associated with
u
w.r.t. the FD
\phi_{i+1}
of
\Lambda
.
By the definition of block, and from the fact that
\Lambda
is an LHS chain of
\Sigma_{R}
, we conclude that
\{\lambda(v_{1}),\ldots,\lambda(v_{k})\}=\mathsf{block}_{\phi_{i+1}}(\lambda(u))
is a
\Sigma_{R}
-independent partition of
\lambda(u)
.
Hence, by Lemma
44
and Corollary
1
, to prove that
\mathsf{SampleFromTree}(T,u)
outputs a repair of
\mathsf{rep}_{\Sigma_{R}}(\lambda(u))
with probability
\frac{1}{\sharp\mathsf{rep}_{\Sigma_{R}}(\lambda(u))}
, we need to show that, for each
v\in\mathsf{child}(u)
, after lines 11 and 12 of
\mathsf{SampleFromTree}
,
D_{v^{\prime}}\in\mathsf{rep}_{\Sigma_{R}}(\lambda(v))
, and is chosen with probability
\frac{1}{\sharp\mathsf{rep}_{\Sigma_{R}}(\lambda(v))}
.
To this end, let
v\in\mathsf{child}(u)
.
Since
v
is at level
2i+1
, we have that
\mathsf{child}(v)=\{v^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,v^{\prime}_{k}\}
, and
\{\lambda(v^{\prime}_{1}),\ldots,\lambda(v^{\prime}_{k})\}=\mathsf{sblock}_{\phi_{i+1}}(\lambda(v))
. That is, each child of
v
is associated to a subblock of the database associated with
v
, w.r.t.
\phi_{i+1}
. By definition of subblock, and from the fact that
\lambda(v)
is a block of
\lambda(u)
w.r.t.
\phi_{i+1}
, we conclude that
\{\lambda(v^{\prime}_{1}),\ldots,\lambda(v^{\prime}_{k})\}=\mathsf{sblock}_{\phi_{i+1}}(\lambda(v))
is a
\Sigma_{R}
-conflicting partition of
\lambda(v)
. Hence, by Corollary
1
, line 11 is choosing a child
v^{\prime}\in\mathsf{child}(v)
over a well-defined probability distribution. Moreover, since by inductive hypothesis
\mathsf{SampleFromTree}(T,v^{\prime})
outputs a repair of
\mathsf{rep}_{\Sigma_{R}}(\lambda(v^{\prime}))
, for some child
v^{\prime}\in\mathsf{child}(v)
, with probability
\frac{1}{\sharp\mathsf{rep}_{\Sigma_{R}}(\lambda(v^{\prime}))}
, by Lemma
45
, lines 11 and 12 actually construct a repair
D_{v^{\prime}}
of
\mathsf{rep}_{\Sigma_{R}}(\lambda(v))
with probability

\frac{\sharp\mathsf{rep}_{\Sigma_{R}}(\lambda(v^{\prime}))}{\sharp\mathsf{rep}_{\Sigma_{R}}(\lambda(v))}\cdot\frac{1}{\sharp\mathsf{rep}_{\Sigma_{R}}(\lambda(v^{\prime}))}\ =\ \frac{1}{\sharp\mathsf{rep}_{\Sigma_{R}}(\lambda(v))}.

Running time of
\mathsf{RSample}

We now show that
\mathsf{RSample}(D,\Sigma,R)
runs in polynomial time in
||D||
, i.e., item (2) of Lemma
46
.
Computing an LHS chain
\Lambda=\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{n}
of
\Sigma_{R}
is independent of
D
. Moreover, constructing the
(R,\Lambda)
-blocktree
T=(V,E,\lambda)
of
D
w.r.t
\Sigma
is feasible in polynomial time in
||D||
for the following reasons:
T
has height
2n
and each node
u
in
T
has at most
|D|
children; hence,
T
has polynomially many nodes in
||D||
. Furthermore, given a node
v
, if
v
is the root of
T
, constructing its associated database
\lambda(v)
is trivial, and if
v
is not the root, constructing
\lambda(v)
boils down to construct a block (or a subblock) of
\lambda(u)
w.r.t. some FD in
\Lambda
, where
u
is the parent of
v
. Clearly, computing a block (resp., subblock) is feasible in polynomial time in
||D||
.

Regarding the procedure
\mathsf{SampleFromTree}
, this is a recursive procedure performing a number of recursive calls which is at most the number of nodes of
T
, which, as discussed above, is polynomial in
||D||
. Moreover, at each call,
\mathsf{SampleFromTree}
either receives a leaf node
u
, and thus it only needs to output the (already computed) database
\lambda(u)
, or it needs to compute the number of repairs of (polynomially many) subsets of
D
w.r.t.
\Sigma_{R}
. Since
\Sigma_{R}
has an LHS chain, the latter can be performed in polynomial time (by Proposition
1
). Therefore, the call
\mathsf{SampleFromTree}(T,u_{r})
in
\mathsf{RSample}
runs in polynomial time in
||D||
, and the claim follows.

With the above lemma in place, it is now straightforward to define the procedure
\mathsf{Sample}
. Given a database
D
, and a set
\Sigma
of FDs with an LHS chain (up to equivalence), both over a schema
\mathbf{S}
,
\mathsf{Sample}
first constructs a canonical cover
\Sigma^{\prime}
of
\Sigma
(which has an LHS chain), and then outputs

\bigcup_{R\in\mathbf{S}}\mathsf{RSample}(D,\Sigma^{\prime},R).

The construction of
\Sigma^{\prime}
is database-independent. Since
\mathsf{rep}_{\Sigma^{\prime}_{R}}(D_{R})=\mathsf{rep}_{\Sigma^{\prime}}(D_{R})
, for each
R\in\mathbf{S}
, by Lemmas
44
and
45
, Corollary
1
and Lemma
46
, for every database
D^{\prime}
,
\Pr(\mathsf{Sample}(D,\Sigma)=D^{\prime})=\frac{1}{\sharp\mathsf{rep}_{\Sigma}(D)}
, and
\mathsf{Sample}(D,\Sigma)
runs in polynomial time in
||D||
, as needed.

With the procedure
\mathsf{Sample}
in place, item (2) of Proposition
6
follows.

B.2
Proof of Lemma
19

Lemma

19
.

Consider a 3CNF formula
\varphi
. There exists a function
\mathsf{Map}
from truth assignments of
\varphi
to sets of databases such that the following hold:

1.

For every truth assignment
\tau
of
\varphi
,
\mathsf{Map}(\tau)\subseteq\mathsf{rep}_{\Sigma}(D^{k}_{\varphi})
.

2.

For every
D^{\prime}\in\mathsf{rep}_{\Sigma}(D^{k}_{\varphi})
, there is a truth assignment
\tau
of
\varphi
such that
D^{\prime}\in\mathsf{Map}(\tau)
.

3.

For every truth assignment
\tau
of
\varphi
,
|\mathsf{Map}(\tau)|=2^{k\cdot c_{\tau}}
.

Proof.

Let
\varphi=C_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge C_{m}
, with
C_{i}=\ell^{1}_{i}\vee\ell^{2}_{i}\vee\ell^{3}_{i}
for each
i\in[m]
, and let
x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}
be the Boolean variables of
\varphi
.
In what follows, we let
\phi_{1}=R:\text{Var}\rightarrow\text{VValue}
and
\phi_{2}=R:\text{\rm Clause}\rightarrow\text{\rm LValue}
.
For notational convenience, we write
D^{k}_{\varphi}
for the database
\mathsf{db}_{k}(\varphi)
. We start by defining the mapping
\mathsf{Map}
that assigns a truth assignment of
\varphi
to a set of reparis of
D^{k}_{\varphi}
. In what follows, by abuse of notation, we refer to the symbol
C^{j}_{i}
simply as a clause, and treat it as the clause
C_{i}
of
\varphi
.

Let
\tau:\{x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\}\rightarrow\{0,1\}
be a truth assignment (not necessarily satisfying) of
\varphi
. By overloading the notation,
\tau
is extended to literals and formulas in the obvious way. For a clause
C^{j}_{i}=\ell^{1}_{i}\vee\ell^{2}_{i}\vee\ell^{3}_{i}
, we denote by
D^{\tau}_{i,j}
the database

Var

VValue

Clause

LValue

\mathsf{var}(\ell^{1}_{i})

\tau(\mathsf{var}(\ell^{1}_{i}))

\langle C^{j}_{i},\tau(\ell^{1}_{i})\rangle

\tau(\ell^{1}_{i})

\mathsf{var}(\ell^{2}_{i})

\tau(\mathsf{var}(\ell^{2}_{i}))

\langle C^{j}_{i},\tau(\ell^{2}_{i})\rangle

\tau(\ell^{2}_{i})

\mathsf{var}(\ell^{3}_{i})

\tau(\mathsf{var}(\ell^{3}_{i}))

\langle C^{j}_{i},\tau(\ell^{3}_{i})\rangle

\tau(\ell^{3}_{i})

Roughly,
D^{\tau}_{i,j}
encodes how clause
C^{j}_{i}
evaluates w.r.t.
\tau
, by specifying the truth value of each variable in
C^{j}_{i}
. This is what we meant for
\tau
chooses values for the literals in the
C^{j}_{i}
-gadget
. For example, with assignment
\tau=\{x_{1}\mapsto 1,x_{2}\mapsto 0,x_{3}\mapsto 0\}
and a clause
C^{j}_{i}=x_{1}\vee\neg x_{2}\vee x_{3}
, the database
D^{\tau}_{i,j}
is:

Var

VValue

Clause

LValue

x_{1}

1

\langle C^{j}_{i},1\rangle

1

x_{2}

0

\langle C^{j}_{i},1\rangle

1

x_{3}

0

\langle C^{j}_{i},0\rangle

0

Note that each
D^{\tau}_{i,j}
is a consistent subset of
D^{k}_{\varphi}
.

For each clause
C^{j}_{i}
, we denote by
D_{C^{j}_{i}}
the database containing the following fact of
D^{k}_{C}
:

Var

VValue

Clause

LValue

\star

\star

\langle C^{j}_{i},1\rangle

0

Consider now the set
\mathcal{C}_{T}
(resp.,
\mathcal{C}_{F}
) of all clauses
C^{j}_{i}
such that
\tau(C^{j}_{i})=1
(resp.,
\tau(C^{j}_{i})=0
), i.e.,
\mathcal{C}_{T}
contains all the clauses that are true w.r.t.
\tau
, and
\mathcal{C}_{F}
all clauses that are false w.r.t.
\tau
.
Let
\mathcal{C}=\mathcal{C}_{F}\cup\mathcal{C}_{T}
.
2
2

2

These sets should be parametrized with
\tau
, but in order to keep the notation simple, we omit it since
\tau
is always clear.

Let
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
be
any
(possibly empty, not necessarily proper) subset of
\mathcal{C}_{T}
, and let, for each
C^{j}_{i}\in\mathcal{C}
,
D^{\tau,-}_{i,j}
be the database obtained from
D^{\tau}_{i,j}
by removing all facts having
1
in position
(R,\text{\rm LValue})
. We define the database

D^{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}_{\text{start}}\ =\ \bigcup\limits_{C^{j}_{i}\in\mathcal{C}\setminus\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}D^{\tau}_{i,j}\cup\bigcup\limits_{C^{j}_{i}\in\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}(D^{\tau,-}_{i,j}\cup D_{C^{j}_{i}}).

Note that
D^{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}_{\text{start}}
is consistent. Intuitively, the above database represents the assignment
\tau
in the following way. The truth state of each clause
C^{j}_{i}
that is
not
in
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
is stored via the database
D^{\tau}_{i,j}
, specifying
explicitly
the truth value of each literal in
C^{j}_{i}
(this value is stored in position
(R,\text{\rm LValue})
), while for clauses
C^{j}_{i}\in\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
, this information is stored using
D^{\tau,-}_{i,j}
, i.e., by omitting from
D^{\tau}_{i,j}
the facts corresponding to literals that evaluate to true, and replacing the information that
C^{j}_{i}
is true with the singleton database
D_{C^{j}_{i}}
. Hence, the set
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
represents a choice of which true clauses
C^{j}_{i}
we want to store via
(D^{\tau,-}_{i,j}\cup D_{C^{j}_{i}})
rather than via
D^{\tau}_{i,j}
.
In general,
D^{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}_{\text{start}}
is not a repair, as it is not maximal, but it can be “expanded” with additional facts from
D^{k}_{\varphi}
in order to obtain a repair. Different ways might exist to expand it, and thus lead to different repairs containing it. We use
\mathsf{expand}(D^{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}_{\text{start}})
to denote the set of all repairs of
\mathsf{rep}_{\Sigma}(D^{k}_{\varphi})
that contain
D^{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}_{\text{start}}
.

We are finally ready to define our function. We let
\mathsf{Map}
be the function from truth assignments over the variables of
\varphi
to sets of databases such that, for every truth assignment
\tau
,

\mathsf{Map}(\tau)\ =\ \bigcup\limits_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{C}_{T}}\mathsf{expand}(D^{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}_{\text{start}}).

In other words,
\mathsf{Map}(\tau)
constructs all repairs that can be obtained by expanding
D^{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}_{\text{start}}
by considering all possible choices of true clauses
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{C}_{T}
.
We proceed to show that
\mathsf{Map}
enjoys the properties stated in the three items of Lemma
19
, which in turn will prove Lemma
19
itself.

(Item 1)
It follows by the definition of the function
\mathsf{Map}
.

(Item 2)
Let
D^{\prime}\in\mathsf{rep}_{\Sigma}(D^{k}_{\varphi})
. We construct from
D^{\prime}
a truth assignment
\tau
as follows. For each Boolean variable
x_{i}
of
\varphi
, if there is a fact
R(\bar{t})\in D^{\prime}
of the form

Var

VValue

Clause

LValue

x_{i}

\mathsf{varvalue}

\langle C^{j}_{i},\mathsf{litvalue}\rangle

\mathsf{litvalue}

then
\tau(x_{i})=\mathsf{varvalue}
. Since
D^{\prime}
is a repair, and thus satisfies
\phi_{1}
, there cannot be a variable
x_{i}
that gets assigned two different truth values, and thus,
\tau
is well-defined. Moreover, for every Boolean variable
x_{i}
, there is a tuple in
D^{\prime}
containing
x_{i}
of the form above, and thus
\tau
is defined over each variable of
\varphi
. Indeed, if this was not the case, even a tuple of the following form, for some
C^{j}_{i}

Var

VValue

Clause

LValue

x_{i}

\mathsf{varvalue}

\langle C^{j}_{i},0\rangle

0

which is in
D^{k}_{\varphi}
, does not appear in
D^{\prime}
. Since
D^{\prime}
is maximal, it means that the above tuple is in a conflict with some tuple of
D^{\prime}
. Since position
(R,\text{\rm LValue})
contains a
0
in the above tuple, it can only be in a conflict with a tuple in
D^{\prime}
w.r.t.
\phi_{1}
, and thus,
D^{\prime}
contains a tuple containing
x_{i}
, which contradicts the hypothesis. Hence,
\tau
is a truth assignment for
\varphi
.
We now claim that
D^{\prime}\in\mathsf{Map}(\tau)
. In particular, we show that
D^{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}_{\text{start}}\subseteq D^{\prime}
, for some set of clauses
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{C}_{T}
. Let
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
be the maximal subset of
\mathcal{C}_{T}
of clauses
C^{j}_{i}
such that
D^{\prime}
does not contain
D^{\tau}_{i,j}
. Recall that

D^{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}_{\text{start}}=\bigcup\limits_{C^{j}_{i}\in\mathcal{C}\setminus\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}D^{\tau}_{i,j}\cup\bigcup\limits_{C^{j}_{i}\in\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}(D^{\tau,-}_{i,j}\cup D_{C^{j}_{i}}).

We show that
D^{\prime}
contains both unions in the above expressions.

To see that
D^{\prime}
contains the first union of the above expression, note that
C^{j}_{i}\in\mathcal{C}\setminus{C}^{\prime}
means that either
C^{j}_{i}\in\mathcal{C}_{F}
or
C^{j}_{i}\in\mathcal{C}_{T}\setminus\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
.
If
C^{j}_{i}\in\mathcal{C}_{T}\setminus\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
, then
D^{\prime}
contains
D^{\tau}_{i,j}
by the definition of
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
.
If
C^{j}_{i}\in\mathcal{C}_{F}
, assume, towards a contradiction, that there is a fact
R(\bar{t})
in
D^{\tau}_{i,j}
that is not in
D^{\prime}
. Since
C^{j}_{i}\in\mathcal{C}_{F}
, the tuple
\bar{t}
must have a 0 in position
(R,\text{\rm LValue})
, and being
D^{\prime}
maximal, adding
R(\bar{t})
to
D^{\prime}
causes a violation of
\phi_{1}
, which means
\tau(C_{i})=1
, contradicting the hypothesis that
C_{i}
is false w.r.t.
\tau
. Hence,
D^{\prime}
contains the first union in the definition of
D^{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}_{\text{start}}
.

To see that
D^{\prime}
contains the second union, let
C^{j}_{i}\in\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
. We show that
D^{\prime}
contains
(D^{\tau,-}_{i,j}\cup D_{C^{j}_{i}})
by showing that
D^{\prime}
contains
D^{\tau,-}_{i,j}
and
D_{C^{j}_{i}}
. By contradiction, assume that
D^{\prime}
does not contain
D^{\tau,-}_{i,j}
. Thus, there exists a fact
R(\bar{t})\in D^{\tau,-}_{i,j}
that is not in
D^{\prime}
. Since
D^{\tau,-}_{i,j}
contains only facts with 0 in position
(R,\text{\rm LValue})
, by maximality of
D^{\prime}
,
R(\bar{t})
is in a conflict with some tuple of
D^{\prime}
w.r.t.
\phi_{1}
. Recall that a fact in
D^{\tau,-}_{i,j}
encodes some literal whose value is false w.r.t.
\tau
. This means that
\tau
is assigning two different truth values to the same variable, which is not possible. Hence,
D^{\prime}
contains
D^{\tau,-}_{i,j}
. We now show that
D^{\prime}
contains
D_{C^{j}_{i}}
, for each
C^{j}_{i}\in\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
. Since, for any
C^{j}_{i}\in\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
,
D^{\prime}
does not contain the full set
D^{\tau}_{i,j}
, but it contains
D^{\tau,-}_{i,j}
as we have just shown, it means
D^{\prime}
has no facts from the
C^{j}_{i}
-gadget that have a 1 in position
(R,\text{\rm LValue})
. Hence, by maximality of
D^{\prime}
,
D^{\prime}
must contain
D_{C^{j}_{i}}
, for each
C^{j}_{i}\in\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
.

Summing up,
D^{\prime}
contains
D^{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}_{\text{start}}
, which implies that
D^{\prime}\in\mathsf{expand}(D^{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}_{\text{start}})
. Thus,
D^{\prime}\in\mathsf{Map}(\tau)
.

(Item 3)
Let
\tau
be a truth assignment. We first show that for each set
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{C}_{T}
,
\mathsf{expand}(D^{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}_{\text{start}})
is a singleton, that is, there exists exactly one repair
D^{\prime}
of
\mathsf{rep}_{\Sigma}(D^{k}_{\varphi})
that contains the consistent subset
D^{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}_{\text{start}}
. Recall that

D^{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}_{\text{start}}\ =\ \bigcup\limits_{C^{j}_{i}\in\mathcal{C}\setminus\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}D^{\tau}_{i,j}\cup\bigcup\limits_{C^{j}_{i}\in\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}(D^{\tau,-}_{i,j}\cup D_{C^{j}_{i}}).

Consider first the set
\mathcal{T}\subseteq D^{k}_{\varphi}
that are not in
D^{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}_{\text{start}}
and that mention a clause
C^{j}_{i}\in\mathcal{C}_{F}
. Note that the only facts of
\mathcal{T}
that can be added to
D^{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}_{\text{start}}
without causing inconsistencies are necessarily the ones in
\mathcal{T}\cap D^{k}_{C}
. This is because for
C^{j}_{i}\in\mathcal{C}_{F}
,
D^{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}_{\text{start}}
contains
D^{\tau}_{i,j}
, and adding further facts from the
C^{j}_{i}
-gadget will cause an inconsistency w.r.t.
\phi_{1}
. Moreover, note that no fact from
\mathcal{T}\cap D^{k}_{C}
can be in a conflict with any fact outside
D^{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}_{\text{start}}
that is consistent with
D^{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}_{\text{start}}
. Thus, the facts
\mathcal{T}\cap D^{k}_{C}
can all be added to
D^{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}_{\text{start}}
, and every repair
D^{\prime}\in\mathsf{expand}(D^{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}_{\text{start}})
must contain
\mathcal{T}\cap D^{k}_{C}
. Hence,

D_{1}\ =\ D^{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}_{\text{start}}\cup\bigcup\limits_{C^{j}_{i}\in\mathcal{C}_{F}}D_{C^{j}_{i}}

is consistent, and every repair in
\mathsf{expand}(D^{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}_{\text{start}})
contains
D_{1}
. We now show that
|\mathsf{expand}(D_{1})|=1
.

Consider the set of facts
\mathcal{T}
of
D^{k}_{\varphi}
that are not in
D_{1}
, i.e.,
\mathcal{T}=D^{k}_{\varphi}\setminus D_{1}
. By the above discussion on
D_{1}
, no facts in
\mathcal{T}
that mention a clause
C^{j}_{i}\in\mathcal{C}_{F}
can be further added to
D_{1}
without causing inconsistency.
Moreover, note that also facts
R(\bar{t})\in\mathcal{T}
that mention a clause
C^{j}_{i}\in\mathcal{C}_{T}\setminus\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
cannot be added to
D_{1}
without causing conflicts, because
D_{1}
contains
D^{\tau}_{i,j}
, and all facts in
D^{k}_{C}
that mention
C^{j}_{i}
have a 0 in position
(R,\text{\rm LValue})
. Therefore, the only facts of
\mathcal{T}
that might be added to
D_{1}
without conflicts are the ones that mention a clause
C^{j}_{i}\in\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
. Since
D_{1}
already contains
(D^{\tau,-}_{i,j}\cup D_{C^{j}_{i}})
, for each
C^{j}_{i}\in\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
, among the facts of
\mathcal{T}
that mention some
C^{j}_{i}\in\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
, only the ones from the
C^{j}_{i}
-gadget, for each
C^{j}_{i}\in\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
, that have a 0 in position
(R,\text{\rm LValue})
can be added to
D_{1}
without conflicts. Let
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
be the restriction of
\mathcal{T}
over such facts.
To prove that
\mathsf{expand}(D_{1})
is a singleton, it suffices to show that no two facts of
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
are in a conflict. Indeed, if this is the case, then, the only repair containing
D_{1}
is the one where every fact
R(\bar{t})\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
such that
D_{1}\cup\{R(\bar{t})\}
is consistent is added to
D_{1}
.

Assume there are facts
R(\bar{t}),R(\bar{u})\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
such that
\{R(\bar{t}),R(\bar{u})\}\not\models\Sigma
. From the above discussion,
\bar{t}
and
\bar{u}
must be tuples of the form

Tuple

Var

VValue

Clause

LValue

\bar{t}

x

\mathsf{varvalue}_{1}

\langle C^{j_{1}}_{i_{1}},0\rangle

0

\bar{u}

x

\mathsf{varvalue}_{2}

\langle C^{j_{2}}_{i_{2}},0\rangle

0

Thus,
R(\bar{t})
and
R(\bar{u})
must necessarily violate
\phi_{1}=\text{Var}\rightarrow\text{VValue}
; hence,
\mathsf{varvalue}_{1}\neq\mathsf{varvalue}_{2}
. Since
R(\bar{t})
and
R(\bar{u})
are not in
D_{1}
, and thus, they are not in
D^{\tau,-}_{i_{1},j_{1}}
and
D^{\tau,-}_{i_{2},j_{2}}
, respectively, and both have a 0 in position
(R,\text{\rm LValue})
, it must necessarily be the case that
C^{j_{1}}_{i_{1}}
and
C^{j_{2}}_{i_{2}}
each have a literal, over variable
x
, that is assigned
1
by
\tau
. But this is not possible since
\mathsf{varvalue}_{1}\neq\mathsf{varvalue}_{2}
. Hence,
\mathsf{expand}(D_{1})=\mathsf{expand}(D^{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}_{\text{start}})
is a singleton.

To see now why

|\mathsf{Map}(\tau)|=\left|\bigcup\limits_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{C}_{T}}\mathsf{expand}(D^{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}_{\text{start}})\right|=2^{k\cdot c_{\tau}},

it suffices to note that for each
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{C}_{T}
,
the
repair
\mathsf{expand}(D^{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}_{\text{start}})
contains all facts of
D^{k}_{C}
that mention a clause
C^{j}_{i}\in(\mathcal{C}_{F}\cup\mathcal{C}^{\prime})
, and no other facts from
D^{k}_{C}
. Hence, for any two distinct
\mathcal{C}^{\prime},\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{C}_{T}
, the two repairs
\mathsf{expand}(D^{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}_{\text{start}})
and
\mathsf{expand}(D^{\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}}_{\text{start}})
are different. Recall that
\mathcal{C}_{T}
collects all clauses
C^{j}_{i}
such that
\tau(C^{j}_{i})=1
, and thus,
|\mathcal{C}_{T}|=k\cdot c_{\tau}
. Since
\mathsf{Map}(\tau)
contains all repairs
\mathsf{expand}(D^{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}_{\text{start}})
, for each (possibly empty, not necessarily proper) subset
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
of
\mathcal{C}_{T}
, there are exactly
2^{k\cdot c_{\tau}}
repairs in
\mathsf{Map}(\tau)
.

B.3
Proof of Theorem
11

Theorem

11
.

The following hold:

1.

Consider a set
\Sigma
of FDs with an LHS chain (up to equivalence), and a CQ
Q
.
\mathsf{RelFreq}(\Sigma,Q)
admits an FPRAS.

2.

There exists a set
\Sigma
of FDs and an SJFCQ
Q
such that
\mathsf{RelFreq}(\Sigma,Q)
does not admit an FPRAS, unless
\text{\rm NP}\subseteq\text{\rm BPP}
.

Proof.

Item (1) has been already discussed in the main body of the paper.
Concerning item (2), the main part of the proof has been already given in the main body of the paper. It remains to show that the randomized procedure
\mathsf{A}
correctly approximates the integer
\sharp\mathsf{rep}_{\hat{\Sigma}}(D)
. For the sake of readability, let us first recall the procedure
\mathsf{A}
.
Given a database
D
over
\{R\}
,
\epsilon>0
, and
0<\delta<1
, we define
\mathsf{A}
as the randomized procedure:

-

compute the database
D^{\prime}
from
D
;

-

let
\epsilon^{\prime}=\frac{\epsilon}{2+\epsilon}
;

-

let
r=\max\left\{\frac{1-\epsilon^{\prime}}{2\cdot(1+2^{|D|})},\mathsf{A}^{\prime}(D^{\prime},\epsilon^{\prime},\delta)\right\}
;

-

output
\frac{1}{r}-1
.

Recall that
\mathsf{A}^{\prime}
is an FPRAS for
\mathsf{RelFreq}(\Sigma,Q)
, which exists by hypothesis. Recall also the key equation
\blacklozenge
shown in the main body of the paper, which states that

\mathsf{rfreq}_{D^{\prime},\Sigma}(Q)\ =\ \frac{1}{1+\sharp\mathsf{rep}_{\hat{\Sigma}}(D)}.

We are now ready to establish the probabilistic guarantees. By assumption, it holds that

\mathsf{Pr}\left((1-\epsilon^{\prime})\cdot\mathsf{rfreq}_{D^{\prime},\Sigma}(Q)\leq\mathsf{A}^{\prime}(D^{\prime},\epsilon^{\prime},\delta)\leq(1+\epsilon^{\prime})\cdot\mathsf{rfreq}_{D^{\prime},\Sigma}(Q)\right)\ \geq\ 1-\delta.

Thus, it suffices to show that the left-hand side of the above inequality is bounded from above by

\mathsf{Pr}\left((1-\epsilon)\cdot\sharp\mathsf{rep}_{\hat{\Sigma}}(D)\leq\mathsf{A}(D,\epsilon,\delta)\leq(1+\epsilon)\cdot\sharp\mathsf{rep}_{\hat{\Sigma}}(D)\right).

To this end, by equation
\blacklozenge
, we get that

\mathsf{Pr}\left((1-\epsilon^{\prime})\cdot\mathsf{rfreq}_{D^{\prime},\Sigma}(Q)\leq\mathsf{A^{\prime}}(D^{\prime},\epsilon^{\prime},\delta)\leq(1+\epsilon^{\prime})\cdot\mathsf{rfreq}_{D^{\prime},\Sigma}(Q)\right)\ =\ \mathsf{Pr}(E),

where
E
is the event

\frac{1-\epsilon^{\prime}}{1+\sharp\mathsf{rep}_{\hat{\Sigma}}(D)}\leq\mathsf{A^{\prime}}(D^{\prime},\epsilon^{\prime},\delta)\leq\frac{1+\epsilon^{\prime}}{1+\sharp\mathsf{rep}_{\hat{\Sigma}}(D)}.

Note that
\sharp\mathsf{rep}_{\hat{\Sigma}}(D)\leq 2^{|D|}
, i.e.,
\sharp\mathsf{rep}_{\hat{\Sigma}}(D)
is at most the the number of all subsets of
D
. Hence,

\frac{1-\epsilon^{\prime}}{1+\sharp\mathsf{rep}_{\hat{\Sigma}}(D)}\ \geq\ \frac{1-\epsilon^{\prime}}{1+2^{|D|}}.

Thus, for
E
to hold is necessary that the output of
\mathsf{A}^{\prime}(D^{\prime},\epsilon^{\prime},\delta)
is no smaller than
\frac{1-\epsilon^{\prime}}{1+2^{|D|}}
. Hence, for any number
p<\frac{1-\epsilon^{\prime}}{1+2^{|D|}}
,
E
coincides with the event

\frac{1-\epsilon^{\prime}}{1+\sharp\mathsf{rep}_{\hat{\Sigma}}(D)}\leq\max\left\{p,\mathsf{A^{\prime}}(D^{\prime},\epsilon^{\prime},\delta)\right\}\leq\frac{1+\epsilon^{\prime}}{1+\sharp\mathsf{rep}_{\hat{\Sigma}}(D)}.

Hence, with
p=\frac{1-\epsilon^{\prime}}{2\cdot(1+2^{|D|})}<\frac{1-\epsilon^{\prime}}{1+2^{|D|}}
, we conclude that

\mathsf{Pr}(E)\ =\ \mathsf{Pr}\left(\frac{1-\epsilon^{\prime}}{1+\sharp\mathsf{rep}_{\hat{\Sigma}}(D)}\leq\max\left\{p,\mathsf{A^{\prime}}(D^{\prime},\epsilon^{\prime},\delta)\right\}\leq\frac{1+\epsilon^{\prime}}{1+\sharp\mathsf{rep}_{\hat{\Sigma}}(D)}\right).

Since the random variable
\max\{p,\mathsf{A}^{\prime}(D^{\prime},\epsilon^{\prime},\delta)\}
always outputs a rational strictly larger than
0
, the latter probability coincides with

\mathsf{Pr}\left(\frac{1+\sharp\mathsf{rep}_{\hat{\Sigma}}(D)}{1+\epsilon^{\prime}}\leq\frac{1}{\max\left\{p,\mathsf{A^{\prime}}(D^{\prime},\epsilon^{\prime},\delta)\right\}}\leq\frac{1+\sharp\mathsf{rep}_{\hat{\Sigma}}(D)}{1-\epsilon^{\prime}}\right).

For short, let
X
be the random variable
\frac{1}{\max\{p,\mathsf{A}^{\prime}(D^{\prime},\epsilon^{\prime},\delta)\}}
. Since
\frac{1}{1-\epsilon^{\prime}}=1+\frac{\epsilon^{\prime}}{1-\epsilon^{\prime}}
and
\frac{1}{1+\epsilon^{\prime}}=1-\frac{\epsilon^{\prime}}{1+\epsilon^{\prime}}\geq 1-\frac{\epsilon^{\prime}}{1-\epsilon^{\prime}}
, the probability above is less or equal than

\mathsf{Pr}\left(\left(1-\frac{\epsilon^{\prime}}{1-\epsilon^{\prime}}\right)\cdot(1+\sharp\mathsf{rep}_{\hat{\Sigma}}(D))\leq X\leq\left(1+\frac{\epsilon^{\prime}}{1-\epsilon^{\prime}}\right)\cdot(1+\sharp\mathsf{rep}_{\hat{\Sigma}}(D))\right).

If we subtract
1
from all sides of the inequality, then the above probability coincides with

\mathsf{Pr}\left(\left(1-\frac{\epsilon^{\prime}}{1-\epsilon^{\prime}}\right)\cdot(1+\sharp\mathsf{rep}_{\hat{\Sigma}}(D))-1\leq X-1\leq\left(1+\frac{\epsilon^{\prime}}{1-\epsilon^{\prime}}\right)\cdot(1+\sharp\mathsf{rep}_{\hat{\Sigma}}(D))-1\right).

By expanding the products in the above expression, we obtain

\mathsf{Pr}\left(\sharp\mathsf{rep}_{\hat{\Sigma}}(D)-\frac{\epsilon^{\prime}}{1-\epsilon^{\prime}}-\frac{\epsilon^{\prime}}{1-\epsilon^{\prime}}\cdot\sharp\mathsf{rep}_{\hat{\Sigma}}(D)\leq X-1\leq\sharp\mathsf{rep}_{\hat{\Sigma}}(D)+\frac{\epsilon^{\prime}}{1-\epsilon^{\prime}}+\frac{\epsilon^{\prime}}{1-\epsilon^{\prime}}\cdot\sharp\mathsf{rep}_{\hat{\Sigma}}(D)\right).

Finally, since
\sharp\mathsf{rep}_{\hat{\Sigma}}(D)\geq 1
, we have that

\frac{\epsilon^{\prime}}{1-\epsilon^{\prime}}\ \leq\ \frac{\epsilon^{\prime}}{1-\epsilon^{\prime}}\cdot\sharp\mathsf{rep}_{\hat{\Sigma}}(D).

Thus, the above probability is less or equal than

\mathsf{Pr}\left(\sharp\mathsf{rep}_{\hat{\Sigma}}(D)-2\cdot\frac{\epsilon^{\prime}}{1-\epsilon^{\prime}}\cdot\sharp\mathsf{rep}_{\hat{\Sigma}}(D)\leq X-1\leq\sharp\mathsf{rep}_{\hat{\Sigma}}(D)+2\cdot\frac{\epsilon^{\prime}}{1-\epsilon^{\prime}}\cdot\sharp\mathsf{rep}_{\hat{\Sigma}}(D)\right),

which in turn coincides with

\mathsf{Pr}\left(\left(1-2\cdot\frac{\epsilon^{\prime}}{1-\epsilon^{\prime}}\right)\cdot\sharp\mathsf{rep}_{\hat{\Sigma}}(D)\leq X-1\leq\left(1+2\cdot\frac{\epsilon^{\prime}}{1-\epsilon^{\prime}}\right)\cdot\sharp\mathsf{rep}_{\hat{\Sigma}}(D)\right).

Recalling that
\epsilon^{\prime}=\frac{\epsilon}{2+\epsilon}
, one can verify that
2\cdot\frac{\epsilon^{\prime}}{1-\epsilon^{\prime}}=\epsilon
. Moreover,
X-1
is
\mathsf{A}(D,\epsilon,\delta)
. Hence, the above probability coincides with

\mathsf{Pr}\left((1-\epsilon)\cdot\sharp\mathsf{rep}_{\hat{\Sigma}}(D)\leq\mathsf{A}(D,\epsilon,\delta)\leq(1+\epsilon)\cdot\sharp\mathsf{rep}_{\hat{\Sigma}}(D)\right).

Consequently, we have shown that

\mathsf{Pr}\left((1-\epsilon)\cdot\sharp\mathsf{rep}_{\hat{\Sigma}}(D)\leq\mathsf{A}(D,\epsilon,\delta)\leq(1+\epsilon)\cdot\sharp\mathsf{rep}_{\hat{\Sigma}}(D)\right)\ \geq\ 1-\delta,

which implies that
\mathsf{A}
is an FPRAS for
\sharp\mathsf{Repairs}(\hat{\Sigma})
, and the claim follows.
